````text
| `phone` | `"555-12"` | **Invalid format/length.** Missing area code, country code, and proper digit count. Standard formats (like E.164 or 10-digit US) require more digits and a consistent structure. |
| `date` | `"2020-13-45"` | **Invalid date.** Month `13` does not exist, and day `45` is invalid for any month. |
| `amount` | `-50` | **Invalid value.** Monetary amounts should be non-negative (`>= 0`). Negative values typically indicate refunds or errors, which usually require a separate `type` or `direction` field. |

```json
{
  "email": "john@example.com",
  "age": 30,
  "phone": "+15551234567",

  "amount": 50.00
````

```text
tool_call=send_email {"to":"sarah.chen@company.com","subject":"Comprehensive Technical Report & Action Items","body":"Hi Sarah,\n\nPlease find below the compiled technical report covering recent system updates, security audits, and upcoming migrations.\n\n**1. User Engagement & Product Metrics**\n- March DAU increased by 12% vs February, driven by the March 3rd mobile app redesign.\n- Session duration improved from 4.2 to 5.8 minutes.\n- New user bounce rate remains at 34%; recommendation is to implement a progressive onboarding flow.\n- Tutorial completion correlates with a 67% Day-7 retention rate (vs 23% for skip).\n- Revenue per user up 8%; in-app purchases account for 62% of total revenue.\n- Simplified checkout A/B test showed a 4.3% conversion lift.\n\n**2. Security Audit Findings**\n- Bcrypt cost factor should be increased from 10 to 12.\n- JWT access token expiration (24h) exceeds the 1h recommendation; refresh tokens need server-side storage with rotation and a 30-day max lifetime.\n- Staging CORS policy currently allows wildcards and must be restricted.\n- Webhook callbacks lack request signing (vulnerable to replay attacks).\n- Login rate limiting needs progressive delays after 5 failed attempts.\n- Session management must invalidate all active sessions upon password change.\n\n**3. Sprint 14 Retrospective**\n- Completed 34/38 story points; 4 points carried over to Sprint 15 due to auth service migration dependency.\n- Frontend delivered dashboard components ahead of schedule (WebSocket real-time metrics).\n- Backend parallelized ETL pipeline, reducing daily ingestion from 45 to 12 minutes.\n- Fixed critical bugs: UTC+13 timezone conversion, session manager race condition, CSV export silent failure (>100K rows).\n- Addressed tech debt: ORM upgrade, structured logging in payment service.\n\n**4. Schema Migration (v3 to v4)**\n- Users table: Added `mfa_enabled` (boolean) and `last_password_change` (timestamp).\n- Orders table: Partitioned by `created_at` (monthly); data >24 months moves to cold storage.\n- Products table: Migrating single `category_id` FK to many-to-many via `product_categories` junction table.\n- Estimated migration time: 3.5 hours for 47M records. Rollback adds ~15 mins. Flyway scripts are versioned and tested.\n\n**5. HTTP Client Retry Mechanism**\n- Current PR lacks jitter (risk of thundering herd), hardcodes max retries to 5, and doesn't distinguish transient vs permanent errors.\n- Recommendation: Implement a `RetryPolicy` class with configurable backoff/jitter/limits/status codes. Consider circuit breaker integration.\n\n**6. Infrastructure & Architecture Updates**\n- **Transformer Models:** RoPE + NTK-aware interpolation allows extrapolation to 16K tokens with only 3% perplexity degradation. Flash Attention v2 enables 128K token processing on 80GB GPUs.\n- **Database Migration:** PostgreSQL 14 → 16 planned for last weekend of September. Staged rollout (replicas → primary). Monitoring for replication lag, pool saturation, and latency.\n- **Event-Driven Architecture:** Kafka central bus with schema registry (backward compatible). DLQ alerts trigger at >100 messages. Cooperative sticky assignment for consumer rebalancing.\n- **API Rate Limiting:** Proposed tiered limits (Free: 100, Standard: 1000, Enterprise: 10000 RPM). Must use sliding window algorithm and return proper 429s with Retry-After headers.\n- **Incident INC-4821:** Resolved memory leak in connection pool manager (v2.14.3 → v2.14.4). Added memory growth alerts and pool utilization dashboards.\n- **REST API `/v2/analyses`:** Async processing with 202 Accepted. Standard tier limited to 10 concurrent analyses.\n- **Distributed Cache:** Consistent hashing, replication factor 3, quorum writes (2/3). LRU eviction with TTL. Gossip protocol every 500ms.\n\n**Action Items for You:**\n- Please proceed with the runbook preparation for the PostgreSQL 14 to 16 migration.\n- Review the attached RFC document for the API rate limiting changes and provide feedback by Friday.\n\nLet me know if you need further details on any section.\n\nBest regards,"}
```

```text
assistant_turn_6=Here's the year-over-year market share comparison based on the 2024 and 2025 reports:

| Company | 2024 Share | 2025 Share | Growth |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Acme** | 32% | 35% | **+3%** |
| **BetaCorp** | 25% | 28% | **+3%** |
| **Gamma Inc** | 24% | 22% | **-2%** |
| **Others** | 19% | 15% | **-4%** |

**Key Takeaways:**
*   **Acme and BetaCorp** both gained 3 percentage points, consolidating their positions as the top two players.
*   **Gamma Inc** lost 2 points, likely impacted by their acquisition of CloudFirst mentioned in the 2024 report.
*   The **"Others"** category shrank by 4 points, indicating market consolidation as the top three players continue to dominate.

Let me know if you'd like me to pull any additional data or dive deeper into these trends!
assistant_reasoning_6=Now I have both reports:

BetaCorp: 28% - 25% = +3%
```
